# Targeted energy discount: results receipt

Great Britain, FY2026-27. Generated by `uk-energy-reforms run`. Datasets:

- `efrs_1573`: Enhanced FRS 2024-25 (policyengine-uk-data 1.57.3) (policyengine/policyengine-uk-data-private@25af520a, sha256 ef34c1ae2821…). Energy spend imputed from LCFS and raked to NEED 2023 at Ofgem Q2-2026 unit rates. Weights are highly concentrated (Kish ESS about 1,100, 2% of its 47,000 GB records) and sum to 30.7m GB households in 2026-27, about 7% above Microcosm (28.6m) and 10% above RF's 28m, so its counts run high.
- `microcosm_national`: Microcosm UK 2024-25 (national release) (policyengine/populace-uk-private@f9d1922c, sha256 aa31bdf67c97…). Energy spend priced with DESNZ QEP FY2024-25, raked to NEED, levelled to Energy Trends. Certified release, published 4 October 2026.

Model: policyengine-uk 2.102.3. Absolute poverty uses the line HBAI has used since March 2026: 60% of the 2024-25 median, held constant in real terms. Relative poverty uses 60% of the baseline UK median, held fixed for the reform.

A dash marks a poverty change resting on fewer than 10 survey records crossing the line. A blanked change is still included in the totals shown: the change for all people, and the national figures for regions and household types.

## Baseline poverty against HBAI

Model rates are for Great Britain in FY2026-27, before the reform. HBAI's are for the UK in 2024-25, the latest year it covers. The model's relative line is 60% of the median in its own data. The numbers of people moved across a line depend on how many sit near it, so they inherit these differences.

| Measure | Group | `microcosm_national` | `efrs_1573` | HBAI |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| abs_pov_bhc | people | 11.5% | 14.4% | 15.9% |
| abs_pov_bhc | children | 13.1% | 16.5% | 20.5% |
| abs_pov_bhc | pensioners | 12.1% | 12.6% | 15.9% |
| abs_pov_ahc | people | 15.3% | 18.7% | 19.6% |
| abs_pov_ahc | children | 19.6% | 24.1% | 27.4% |
| abs_pov_ahc | pensioners | 10.4% | 11.4% | 13.9% |
| rel_pov_bhc | people | 13.9% | 19.3% | 15.9% |
| rel_pov_bhc | children | 16.3% | 23.3% | 20.5% |
| rel_pov_bhc | pensioners | 15.2% | 20.8% | 15.9% |
| rel_pov_ahc | people | 18.3% | 23.3% | 19.6% |
| rel_pov_ahc | children | 23.8% | 31.6% | 27.4% |
| rel_pov_ahc | pensioners | 14.8% | 16.7% | 13.9% |

## Passporting against benefit caseloads

- `microcosm_national`: 7.55m passported households (26.4% of GB).
- `efrs_1573`: 7.97m passported households (26.0% of GB).
- DWP: 7.2m benefit units on Universal Credit, 6.7m of them with a payment (GB, May 2026, provisional); 1.38m Pension Credit claimants (GB, March 2026).
- The caseloads count benefit units and overlap where one household holds both, so they bound the passported count loosely; Housing Benefit and the legacy benefits add a little more.

## Gas spend against DESNZ

DESNZ's average domestic gas bill at actual consumption was GBP 773 per on-gas household in 2024 (GBP 841 at temperature-adjusted consumption; GBP 752 in 2025), with 89% of GB households on gas: GBP 19.3bn in total (Quarterly Energy Prices table 2.3.5). That share is DESNZ's estimate of on-gas households over GB households. The 84% quoted elsewhere is a different basis: gas meters over all domestic properties, which include empty and second homes (sub-national consumption statistics). The reconciliation uses the household basis, as the bills do. Model figures are for FY2026-27 households at each dataset's stored price level: 2024-25 for Microcosm, April-June 2026 unit rates for the Enhanced FRS.

| Dataset | Households with gas spend | Gas spend per such household (GBP) | Against DESNZ | Total gas spend (GBP bn) | Against DESNZ |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `microcosm_national` | 78.6% | 665 | -14% | 14.9 | -23% |
| `efrs_1573` | 94.7% | 725 | -6% | 21.1 | +9% |

## Take-up sensitivity

Every figure elsewhere assumes that every eligible household receives the discount. Here households eligible through the income test alone take it up at the given rate; passported households are enrolled automatically. Expected values from the full take-up run (`analysis.take_up_sensitivity`). Poverty columns are the change in people.

## Income cut-offs

Households near each income threshold where support falls, counting only households placed on the schedule by their own income. The offset range holds households above a threshold whose extra income, after tax, is smaller than the support they lose there. Options without an income test have no cut-offs. By region, each option's tables below give the count within GBP 1k above the top threshold and the offset range.

**rf_flat**:

- `microcosm_national`, GBP 24,000: mean drop GBP 175; GBP 1k below 342k (228k lowest-4); GBP 1k above 278k (120k lowest-4, 62k in rel. AHC poverty; ESS 57); offset range 64k (median width GBP 243).
- `efrs_1573`, GBP 24,000: mean drop GBP 175; GBP 1k below 421k (279k lowest-4); GBP 1k above 410k (270k lowest-4, 101k in rel. AHC poverty; ESS 11); offset range 49k (median width GBP 243).
